|------|-------------|
| `DNAC_STORAGE_STAKE_MIN` (10^14 raw = 1M NODUS) | **NEW; REMOVED by HF-6 6b-1** (→ `DNAC_HF6_STORAGE_BOND_DEFAULT` / `_MIN` / `_MAX`, §5b.12). The storage registration bond, EXACTLY (the STORAGE_REGISTER exec refuses any other). |
| `DNAC_CFG_RULESET_GEN_STORAGE` (16), `DNAC_CFG_PARAM_MAX_ID` (= 16) | **NEW / CHANGED.** The rule-set upgrade vote to GEN_STORAGE (after Nodus EVM's 14 / 15); on `dnac_cfg_param_read_by_consensus`. `chain_config_cache` grows to 17 rows through its `DNAC_CFG_PARAM_MAX_ID` derivation. |
| `DNAC_CFG_RULESET_GEN_STORAGE_D` | **NEW.** The vote's only legal value: `dna_ruleset_gen_digest(4, GEN_STORAGE SYSTEM hash, GEN_STORAGE CORE hash, DNAC_RULESET_SWITCH_SPEC_VERSION)`; **0 until the oracle fills it**. |
| `NODUS_RT_GEN_STORAGE` ((uint32_t)4), `NODUS_RT_GEN_STORAGE_BASE` (= `NODUS_RT_GEN_EVM`), `NODUS_RT_GEN_MAX` (= `NODUS_RT_GEN_STORAGE` under `NODUS_EVM_ENABLED`, else `NODUS_RT_GEN_2`) | **NEW / CHANGED.** The fourth compiled generation, on the EVM generation (SYSTEM v9 rules {1..9} / CORE v7 rules {1..9} incl. EVMFUND, policy = the EVM generation's ops 1..9 weight 1; NO EVM slot — EVM v1 unchanged, its tuple stays the EVM generation's). |
| `DNA_SYSRULE_STORAGE_REGISTER` (7), `DNA_SYSRULE_STORAGE_EXIT` (8), `DNA_SYSRULE_STORAGE_REPORT` (9) | **NEW.** SYSTEM rule ids owned by GEN_STORAGE only. Op 9 is refused by the hooks until package B2. |
| `NODUS_STORAGE_SET_MAX` (= `DNA_V2_STORAGE_SET_MAX`, 256) | **NEW.** The cap STORAGE_REGISTER admits against (live rows = ACTIVE + EXITING). |

### 24.5 Archive reward, package B2a (`nodus/src/witness/nodus_witness_v2_storage.{h,c}` NEW; decisions `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-05-storage-reward-is-for-archive.md`, `2026-10-05-archive-reward-bytes-approved.md`)

|----------|-------------|

| `int nodus_witness_storage_boundary_apply(nodus_witness_t *w, uint64_t boundary_height, const uint8_t chain_id[32], nodus_storage_boundary_t *out)` | **NEW.** Step 1b' of the epoch boundary: gate (param 16 in effect at B, then SYSTEM runtime ≥ GEN_STORAGE else FAULT), settle (H = B − 2E; fail_streak through `nodus_storage_fail_streak_next` — K5a: weight and OK resets to 0 even when skipped; K5: otherwise a skipped member adds one at every settled epoch and returns at 0 after 12; K9: a member in grace is left unchanged), prune, release EXITING bonds, publish due segments, freeze storage_set(B) with the K9 grace update (§24.9). 0 (or gate closed, `active` 0) / -2 FAULT. |
| `int nodus_witness_storage_publish_due(nodus_witness_t *w, uint64_t boundary_height, uint32_t *n_out)` | **NEW.** Step 4 alone: every unpublished k with k·17280 + 2E ≤ B, from the last published + 1; Root(k) from `v2_blocks.block_id`; a missing / malformed row stops with -1 before its row. |

| `int nodus_witness_storage_reports_root(nodus_witness_t *w, uint8_t out[64])` | **NEW.** reports_root over `v2_storage_reports` ((epoch_start, seat) ASC). |
| `int nodus_witness_storage_segments_root(nodus_witness_t *w, uint8_t out[64])` | **NEW.** segments_root over `v2_storage_segments` (k ASC, contiguous 1..max checked). |
| `int nodus_witness_storage_set_get(nodus_witness_t *w, uint64_t epoch_start, nodus_storage_set_t *out)` | **NEW.** One frozen set; 0 / 1 absent / -1 malformed (stored S(H) re-derived). |
| `int nodus_witness_storage_segment_root_compute(nodus_witness_t *w, uint64_t k, uint8_t out[64])` | **NEW.** Root(k) from `v2_blocks`; missing / non-64-byte rows → -1. |
| `int nodus_witness_storage_holders(const nodus_storage_set_t *set, const uint8_t seg_root[64], uint8_t out_fps[3][64], size_t *n_out)` | **NEW.** holders(k, X) over a frozen set, ascending distance. |
| `int nodus_witness_storage_eligible_segments(nodus_witness_t *w, uint64_t epoch_start, const uint8_t node_fp[64], uint64_t *ks_out, size_t cap, size_t *n_out)` | **NEW; CHANGED (behaviour) by K9.** The eligible segments of a member of set(H) in epoch (H, H+E], k ascending — none while H < its frozen grace_until; else every k with (published ≤ H and m ∈ holders(k, set(H))) or (published ≤ H−E and m ∈ holders(k, set(H−E))) — `nodus_storage_member_eligible` (§24.9). Until K9: published ≤ H−E and in holders(k, set(H−E)) only (G = 1). 0 / 1 no set(H) / -1. The ONE rule of the settlement weights, the reporter's probe list and the probed node's answer. |
| `uint32_t nodus_storage_fail_streak_next(uint32_t old, int had_eligible, int ok)` | **NEW (pure, K5 + K5a).** fail_streak after one SETTLED epoch (bytes item 4 + decisions K5, K5a), first matching line: an eligible block and OK → 0 whatever old (K5a); else old ≥ 3 (`DNA_V2_STORAGE_FAIL_LIMIT`, skipped) → old + 1, 14 → 0 (`NODUS_STORAGE_FAIL_RETURN` 15 is never stored); else an eligible block and NOT OK → old + 1; else unchanged. Called by the settlement for every member of set(H); not called under a failed F1 floor. |
| `int nodus_storage_eligible_height(const uint64_t *ks, size_t n, uint64_t position, uint64_t *height_out)` | **NEW (pure).** Position in the eligible blocks → height. |
| `int nodus_witness_v2_accrue(nodus_witness_t *w, const uint8_t owner_fp[64], uint64_t x)` (`nodus_witness_v2_econ.{h,c}`) | **NEW (export).** The accrual writer `v2ec_accrue`, exported so the storage settlement credits through the same path. 0 / -2. |
| `int nodus_witness_v2_epoch_release_utxo(nodus_witness_t *w, const uint8_t nullifier[64], const uint8_t tx_hash[64], uint32_t output_index, const uint8_t *owner_fp128, uint64_t amount, uint64_t block_height, uint64_t unlock_block)` (`nodus_witness_v2_epoch.{h,c}`) | **NEW (export).** The boundary's one release-UTXO writer (`v2ep_release_utxo`), now also used by the storage exit release; values above INT64_MAX → -2. |
| `nodus_v2_epoch_result_t` | **CHANGED:** + `storage_accrued`, `n_storage_releases` — engine phase 6e declares CORE touched also when either is nonzero. |
| `int nodus_witness_v2_epoch_boundary_apply(...)` | **CHANGED (behaviour):** calls `nodus_witness_storage_boundary_apply` between the validator distribution and payday. |

### 24.6 Archive reward, package B2b-1 — the archive probe's node side (`nodus/src/witness/nodus_witness_storage_probe.{h,c}` and `nodus_witness_storage_reporter.{h,c}` NEW; design `docs/plans/2026-10-05-archive-reward-design.md` rev 4 §4; request bytes `docs/plans/2026-10-05-archive-reward-bytes.md` §6)

OFF-CHAIN: nothing here writes state; a probe reaches state only through the reporter's own signed STORAGE_REPORT (design rev 4 §7 D3). Wire: channel `0x72` on 4004 (byte pending operator approval), message = `kind(1) ‖ body`; kind 1 REQUEST = the 288-byte §6 request; kind 2 ANSWER = `rq[64] ‖ code(1) ‖ (code 0: 3 × (hdr_len u32 ‖ header(h+1) proto ‖ part_len u32 ‖ part ‖ proof_len u32 ‖ proof proto))`.

| Function | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| `NODUS_STPROBE_REQ_LEN` (288), `NODUS_STPROBE_REQ_MSG_LEN` (289), `NODUS_STPROBE_REFUSAL_LEN` (66), `NODUS_STPROBE_HEADER_MAX` (2048), `NODUS_STPROBE_PROOF_MAX` (8192), `NODUS_STPROBE_MSG_MAX`, `NODUS_STPROBE_BUDGET_MS` (10000), `NODUS_STPROBE_SERVE_GAP_MS` (2000), `NODUS_STPROBE_MAX_SEGS` (4096), `NODUS_STPROBE_REPORT_*` | **NEW.** Wire sizes and bounds (the answer is ≤ `NODUS_STPROBE_MSG_MAX` ≈ 227 KB, the 0x72 receive capacity), the answer budget (⚠ not grounded — design §9 leaves the deadline open), the serving gap, the segment cap, the report envelope declarations. |
| `nodus_stprobe_code_t` | **NEW.** One-byte answer code: 0 OK; refusals MALFORMED, NOT_ADDRESSED, WRONG_CHAIN, LATE, NOT_REPORTER, UNKNOWN_SET, SET_MISMATCH, NOT_MEMBER, NO_BLOCKS, NOT_HELD, RATE, NOT_SEATED, FAULT. |
| `nodus_stprobe_req_t { chain_id[32]; epoch_start; set_hash[64]; target_fp[64]; reporter_fp[64]; nonce[32]; deadline_ms; }` | **NEW.** The §6 request. |
| `int nodus_stprobe_req_encode(const nodus_stprobe_req_t *req, uint8_t out[289])` / `int nodus_stprobe_req_decode(const uint8_t *msg, size_t len, nodus_stprobe_req_t *out)` | **NEW.** kind 1 ‖ `"NDS.STPROBE.v2"` padded to 16 ‖ fields in §6 order, integers BE. Decode: exact length, kind, the padded tag. 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_stprobe_req_id(const nodus_stprobe_req_t *req, uint8_t rq[64])` | **NEW.** rq = SHA3-512(the 288 body bytes). |
| `nodus_stprobe_code_t nodus_stprobe_req_check(const nodus_stprobe_req_t *req, const uint8_t own_fp[64], const uint8_t own_chain[32], const uint8_t sender_fp[64], uint64_t E, uint64_t now_wall_ms)` | **NEW (pure).** Serving-side checks without a database: chain, addressed to this node, reporter == SHA3-512(the sender's authenticated key), epoch_start a boundary ≥ E, not past the deadline. |
| `int nodus_stprobe_samples(const uint8_t nonce[32], const uint8_t target_fp[64], const uint64_t *ks, size_t n, uint8_t x_out[3][64], uint64_t h_out[3])` | **NEW (pure).** x_i (`dna_v2_storage_sample_x`) and the sampled heights over the eligible segments (B = n × 17280). n == 0 → -1. |
| `nodus_stprobe_sample_view_t`, `nodus_stprobe_ans_view_t` | **NEW.** A decoded answer (pointers into the message). |
| `int nodus_stprobe_ans_refusal(const uint8_t rq[64], uint8_t code, uint8_t out[66])` / `int nodus_stprobe_ans_decode(const uint8_t *msg, size_t len, nodus_stprobe_ans_view_t *out)` | **NEW.** Refusal = kind ‖ rq ‖ code; an OK answer must carry exactly 3 bounded samples and no trailing byte. |
| `nodus_stprobe_code_t nodus_stprobe_answer_build(nodus_cmt_store_t *store, const uint8_t rq[64], const uint8_t x[3][64], const uint64_t h[3], uint64_t B, uint8_t *out, size_t cap, size_t *len_out)` | **NEW.** The serving side's answer from the block store: meta of h+1 (its header, marshalled), part index from x and that header's last_block_id part count, the stored part P:h:i and its proof. OK / NOT_HELD / FAULT. |
| `nodus_stprobe_verify_t nodus_stprobe_verify_sample(const uint8_t x[64], uint64_t B, uint64_t h, const uint8_t hash_h[64], const uint8_t hash_h1[64], const nodus_stprobe_sample_view_t *s)` | **NEW.** The §6 chain against this node's own v2_blocks: header hash == hash[h+1], height h+1, last_block_id.hash == hash[h], part count 1..1601, proof decodes + ValidateBasic, proof.index == sampled part, proof.total == part count, Part.ValidateBasic, `cmt_proof_verify`. A distinct code per failing link. |
| `bool nodus_stprobe_answer_ok(const nodus_stprobe_ans_view_t *a, const uint8_t expect_rq[64], const uint8_t x[3][64], uint64_t B, const uint64_t h[3], const uint8_t hash_h[3][64], const uint8_t hash_h1[3][64], int64_t now_mono_ms, int64_t deadline_mono_ms)` | **NEW.** OK iff not late, rq matches, code 0 and all three samples verify. |
| `int nodus_stprobe_report_call(uint64_t epoch_start, uint32_t seat, const uint8_t set_hash[64], const bool *ok, uint32_t count, uint8_t out[110], size_t *len_out)` | **NEW.** The STORAGE_REPORT call (2026-10-04 bytes item 3): bit i LSB-first = member i, `bitmap_len` = ⌈count/8⌉. |
| `int nodus_stprobe_report_env(const uint8_t chain32[32], uint32_t sys_version, const uint8_t sys_hash[64], uint64_t applying_height, uint64_t expiry, const uint8_t *call, size_t call_len, const uint8_t *pk, const uint8_t *sk, uint8_t **out, size_t *out_len)` | **NEW.** The 1-leg SYSTEM op-9 envelope, fee 0, units 400000, one kind-1 signer (the seat key = this node's identity), signed over the leg's auth digest at `applying_height`. `*out` heap. |
| `int nodus_stprobe_epoch_for_tip(uint64_t tip, uint64_t E, uint64_t *H_out)`, `uint64_t nodus_stprobe_slot_height(uint64_t H, uint64_t E, size_t j, size_t n)`, `int nodus_stprobe_report_window(uint64_t H, uint64_t E, uint64_t tip, uint64_t *expiry_out)` | **NEW (pure).** Probing epoch H = ⌊tip/E⌋·E; target j's first slot H + ⌊j·⌊3E/4⌋/n⌋; the report window (H+E, H+E+⌊E/2⌋] over tip + 1 (0 open / 1 not yet / -1 closed), expiry = min(tip + 100, close). |
| `nodus_stprobe_code_t nodus_witness_stprobe_serve(nodus_witness_t *w, nodus_cmt_store_t *store, const uint8_t sender_fp[64], const nodus_stprobe_req_t *req, uint64_t E, uint64_t now_wall_ms, uint8_t *out, size_t cap, size_t *len_out)` | **NEW.** The serving side's whole decision: pure checks, storage_set(H) here with the request's S(H), this node a member with B > 0 (its OWN eligible segments — a peer cannot make it read other blocks), the requester seated in snapshot(H), then the answer. |
| `void nodus_witness_stprobe_tick(nodus_witness_t *w)` | **NEW.** The reporter, at most every 500 ms on a live caught-up version-3 lane: opens the probing epoch once per H when seated in snapshot(H) and set(H) is non-empty; probes each other member at its slot over the existing 4004 connection (no dial); at tip ≥ H+E closes it; inside the window submits the signed report to its own mempool (`cmt_mem_check_tx`) and resubmits after expiry / a refused CheckTx (10 blocks). |
| `int nodus_witness_stprobe_on_msg(nodus_witness_t *w, const char *peer_id, const uint8_t sender_fp[64], const uint8_t *msg, size_t len)` | **NEW.** A 0x72 message: REQUEST → served (rate gap, refusal codes answered); ANSWER → taken only by an outstanding probe with the same rq from the same 64-byte identity. -1 = undecodable (the host stops the peer). |
| `void nodus_witness_stprobe_free(nodus_witness_t *w)` | **NEW.** Frees the runtime (witness close). |
| `nodus_witness_t.stprobe` (`struct nodus_stprobe_rt *`) | **NEW field.** The probe runtime, runtime only. |
| `NODUS_P2P_CH_STPROBE` (0x72), `NODUS_P2P_STPROBE_PRIORITY` (1), `NODUS_P2P_STPROBE_SEND_QUEUE` (4) (`nodus_witness_p2p.h`) | **NEW.** The channel and its descriptor (receive capacity `NODUS_STPROBE_MSG_MAX`); listed in NodeInfo; reactor "STPROBE". `peer_fp` (static, p2p.c) — SHA3-512 of a peer's authenticated key, 64 bytes; `peer_wid` now delegates to it. |
| `void nodus_witness_tick(nodus_witness_t *w)` / `void nodus_witness_close(nodus_witness_t *w)` | **CHANGED (behaviour):** the tick calls `nodus_witness_stprobe_tick` after the consensus lane on a version-3 chain; the close frees the probe runtime before the p2p host. |

### 24.7 Archive reward, package B2b-CLI — `nodus-cli storage register | exit | status` (shared builder ops 7 / 8, `dnac_storage_status`; decisions `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-05-storage-reward-is-for-archive.md`, `2026-09-25-web-wallet-nodus-send-transport.md` "İşlem kurucu"; call bytes `docs/plans/2026-10-04-storage-reward-bytes.md` item 5)

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `NODUS_V2_STAKE_OP_STORAGE_REGISTER` (= 7 = `DNA_SYSRULE_STORAGE_REGISTER`), `NODUS_V2_STAKE_OP_STORAGE_EXIT` (= 8 = `DNA_SYSRULE_STORAGE_EXIT`), `NODUS_V2_STREG_CALL_LEN` (2664u), `NODUS_V2_STEXIT_CALL_LEN` (2592u) (`nodus_v2_stake.h`) | **NEW; the layouts below REPLACED by HF-6 6b-1** (7299u / 64u, owner-signed, node consent — §5b.12). Signatures of `nodus_v2_stake_build` / `nodus_v2_stake_decode` unchanged; both accept ops 7 and 8. REGISTER call = node_pk[2592] ‖ bond u64 BE ‖ payee_fp[64] (`RTN_SYS_STREG_CALL_LEN`, `rtn_streg_parse`), the request's `amount` is the bond and `dest_fp` the payee, funding lock = bond. EXIT call = the node key (`RTN_SYS_STEXIT_CALL_LEN`), `amount` / `dest_fp` ignored, fee-only funding, decoded `amount` 0. Built against the caller's tuples — the ops exist from `NODUS_RT_GEN_STORAGE` only. `NODUS_V2_STAKE_ERR_OP` now means "not op 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 7 / 8". |
| `NODUS_V2_STAKE_ERR_STORAGE_BOND` (−44), `NODUS_V2_STAKE_ERR_PAYEE` (−45) | **NEW; HF-6 6b-1: −44 now means bond ∉ [1, INT64_MAX], −45 REMOVED (§5b.12).** REGISTER bond ≠ `DNAC_STORAGE_STAKE_MIN`; REGISTER payee ≠ SHA3-512(pk) (the exec's pre-HF-5 rule, `rtn_storage_register_exec`). `nodus_v2_stake_decode` also refuses a REGISTER whose payee ≠ SHA3-512(node_pk). |
| `NODUS_DNAC_STORAGE_SET_MAX` (256u), `NODUS_DNAC_STORAGE_SEG_MAX` (64u), `nodus_dnac_storage_status_t { committed_height; epoch_start; found; status; bond; fail_streak; grace_until; registered_height; exit_height; payee[129]; set_exists; set_count; member; n_segments; n_listed; segments[64]; }` (`nodus.h`) | **NEW; `grace_until` added by K9.** The `dnac_storage_status` reply (wire in `nodus.h`): keys `ch`, `es`, `found`, row keys `st` / `bond` / `fs` / `gu` (K9, u64) / `rh` / `xh` / `payee` iff found, `set`, `sc`, `mem`, `ns`, `segs` (first min(ns, 64), k ascending). |
| `int nodus_client_dnac_storage_status(nodus_client_t *client, const char *node_fp_hex, nodus_dnac_storage_status_t *out)` | **NEW.** `node_fp_hex` = 128 lowercase hex (checked before sending). 0 / the node's `NODUS_ERR_*` (older node: "unknown DNAC method") / `NODUS_ERR_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (reply refused by the decoder) / `NODUS_ERR_TIMEOUT` / −1 invalid args or transport. |
| `int nodus_dnac_storage_status_decode(const uint8_t *raw, size_t raw_len, nodus_dnac_storage_status_t *out)` | **NEW.** Exported for tests. v3d_* discipline (duplicate key / truncation / wrong type refuse; unknown keys skipped); every key required, row keys (7 since K9, `gu` any u64) iff `found`; `st` 1..3, `rh` ≥ 1, `fs` ≤ u32, `sc` ≤ 256, `payee` lowercase hex; `es` ≤ `ch`; no set ⇒ `sc` 0 and not a member; member ⇒ found; not a member ⇒ `ns` 0; list length = min(ns, 64), entries ≥ 1 strictly ascending. 0 / −1 (`out` zeroed). |
| `static void handle_dnac_storage_status(nodus_witness_t *w, struct nodus_tcp_conn *conn, const uint8_t *payload, size_t len, uint32_t txn_id)`, `static int st_status_row(nodus_witness_t *w, const uint8_t fp[64], nodus_dnac_storage_status_t *o)` (`nodus_witness_handlers.c`) | **NEW (internal).** Method `dnac_storage_status` (reaches the witness through the generic `dnac_` prefix route — no protocol change). Reads committed tables only: the `v2_storage_nodes` row (typed checks; a malformed row is a fault), `storage_set(H)` with H = tip − tip mod E (`nodus_witness_storage_set_get`), the eligible segments (`nodus_witness_storage_eligible_segments`, list sized tip / 17280 + 1, sent truncated to 64 with the full count). Bad `fp` → `PROTOCOL_ERROR`; no version-3 chain → `NOT_FOUND`; any store fault, a member without a registry row, or an overflow → `INTERNAL_ERROR`, never a partial answer. The last settled outcome is not returned: the settlement keeps no per-member verdict. |
| `static int cmd_storage_tx(const char *server_ip, uint16_t server_port, int argc, char **argv, int cmd_start, int is_exit)`, `static int cmd_storage_status(const char *server_ip, uint16_t server_port, int argc, char **argv, int cmd_start, int have_identity)`, `static int cli_storage_session(nodus_client_t *client, int *connected, const char *submit, const char *server_ip, uint16_t server_port)`, `static void cli_storage_status_print(const char *fp_hex, const nodus_dnac_storage_status_t *s)`, `static const char *cli_storage_status_name(uint8_t s)` (`nodus-cli.c`) | **NEW (internal); HF-6 6b-1: `cmd_storage_tx` REMOVED (→ `cmd_storage_tx_refused`, §5b.12) — register / exit are no longer built; HF-6 6b-3 (§5b.14): `storage consent | register | exit` are the owner-side commands (`cmd_validator_consent`, `cmd_v2_stake`), `cli_storage_status_print` prints the row's `payee` as `owner:`.** `nodus-cli -i <node dir> storage register \| exit (--dry-run \| --submit ip:port)` and `storage status [--fp <hex128>]`. register / exit (until 6b-1): the node identity (`-i` required) signs and funds; chain id, generation (refused below `NODUS_RT_GEN_STORAGE`), the registry row (register refused when ACTIVE / EXITING, exit refused when absent or not ACTIVE; a failed query is a warning), gas price, coins and tip on one session; built with `nodus_v2_stake_build`; prints what is sent before the wire / intent ids. status: the generation, then the row (since K9 with grace_until, and "IN GRACE this epoch … N grace epoch(s) left" while es < gu), the frozen-set membership for the current epoch, the eligible segments, and "last settled outcome: not recorded on chain". |
| `test_storage_cli` (`nodus/tests/test_storage_cli.c`) | **NEW test.** Builder envelopes for ops 7 / 8 through the GEN_STORAGE SYSTEM and CORE hooks (read plans, records, funding equation), builder / decoder refusals, the status decoder's rules. |

### 24.8 Archive reward, package B2b-2 — segment FILES and the 0x73 FETCH (`nodus/src/witness/nodus_witness_storage_segment.{h,c}` + `nodus_witness_storage_segment_export.c` since P-B4 §24.11, `nodus_witness_storage_fetch.{h,c}`, `nodus_witness_storage_holder.{h,c}` NEW; design `docs/plans/2026-10-05-archive-reward-design.md` rev 4 §2, §3; bytes doc §6 chain, §7 file; decisions `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-05-storage-reward-is-for-archive.md`, `2026-10-05-kurultay-7-archive-reward-summary.md`, `2026-10-03-block-pruning-7-paydays.md`)

NODE-LOCAL: no file, fetch or clock here is a consensus input (design rev 4 §7 D3, D4); every byte written is verified first against the build's trusted block hashes — `v2_blocks.block_id` through the ledger hash source (P-B1). Layouts: `nodus/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` "package B2b-2". **Since storage P-B4 (nodus 0.26.16, §24.11)** the file is split in two: `nodus_witness_storage_segment.c` (format, build, resume, reader, deletion — every file call through `storage/nodus_fs.h`) is in the COMMON source list (`nodus/CMakeLists.txt:354`) and compiles for Windows; `nodus_witness_storage_segment_export.c` (`nodus_seg_store_has`, `nodus_seg_export_step`, `nodus_seg_probe_sample`, `nodus_seg_valset_from_store`) is in the Linux-only list (`nodus/CMakeLists.txt:765`, inside `if(NOT WIN32)` from :644 — lines as of storage P-B2). All declarations stay in `nodus_witness_storage_segment.h`; a Windows build links none of the four store- / witness-backed functions — a call there is a link error, never a silent stub (`nodus_witness_storage_segment.h:459-466`). ⚠ Channel `0x73` and its wire (including the K8a set selector `0xFFFFFFFE`) are PENDING operator approval. Decision K8a (2026-10-05, replaces K8): every segment file carries validators(k·17280) and its terminal commit's signatures are ALWAYS verified against it — a file without them is not complete.

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `NODUS_SEG_TAG_LEN` (16), `NODUS_SEG_FILE_TAG` ("NDS.SEGFILE.v1"), `NODUS_SEG_INDEX_TAG` ("NDS.SEGINDEX.v1"), `NODUS_SEG_DONE_TAG` ("NDS.SEGDONE.v1"), `NODUS_SEG_COUNT` (17280), `NODUS_SEG_DATA_HDR_LEN` (28), `NODUS_SEG_INDEX_ENTRY_LEN` (12), `NODUS_SEG_INDEX_LEN` (207420 — K8a added valset_off u64 ‖ valset_len u32), `NODUS_SEG_DONE_LEN` (105), `NODUS_SEG_FLAG_SIGS` (0x01 — K8a: the marker's flags byte is always exactly this), `NODUS_SEG_HEADER_MAX` (2048), `NODUS_SEG_PART_PROTO_MAX` (65536 + 8192 + 64), `NODUS_SEG_COMMIT_MAX` (599839), `NODUS_SEG_VALIDATOR_PB_MAX` (2657), `NODUS_SEG_VALSET_MAX` (342764), `NODUS_SEG_PATH_MAX` (512), `NODUS_SEG_PART_COMMIT` (0xFFFFFFFF), `NODUS_SEG_PART_VALSET` (0xFFFFFFFE) | **NEW.** The file layout constants (node-local, not consensus); the commit bound is MaxCommitBytes at 128 validators; the set bound (K8a) is (128 + 1) framed Validator messages of at most 2657 bytes (address 34 + pub_key 2598 + power 11 + priority 11, + tag and 2-byte length) + total_voting_power 11. |
| `nodus_seg_v_t` + `const char *nodus_seg_v_str(nodus_seg_v_t v)` | **NEW.** One code per failing verification link (ORDER, BOUNDS, NO_LEDGER, HEADER_DECODE / _HASH / _HEIGHT, BLOCK_ID, PART_TOTAL / _DECODE / _INDEX, PROOF_TOTAL, PART_BASIC, PROOF, COMMIT_DECODE / _HEIGHT / _BLOCK_ID / _HASH / _VALSET (the set does not hash to header(k·P).validators_hash) / _SIGS, VALSET_DECODE (K8a: the set proto does not decode or ValidatorSetFromProto refuses it), IO, FAULT) and its log name. |
| `nodus_seg_file_t`, `int nodus_seg_path(const char *dir, uint64_t k, nodus_seg_file_t which, char *out, size_t cap)`, `int nodus_seg_name_parse(const char *name, uint64_t *k_out)`, `int nodus_seg_dir_ensure(const char *dir)`, `uint64_t nodus_seg_of_height(uint64_t h)` | **NEW.** `dir/seg-<k>.{dat.tmp,dat,idx.tmp,idx,ok.tmp,ok}` (k decimal, no padding); the parser refuses every other name (leading zero, k 0, other suffix — so a Windows unlink leftover `seg-<k>.dat.deleted.<pid>.<n>` is never a segment file, §24.11); `dir_ensure`: an existing directory → 0, an existing non-directory → -1, else one level created — since P-B4 through `nodus_fs_is_dir` + `nodus_fs_mkdir` (POSIX `stat` + `S_ISDIR`, `mkdir(path, 0700)`, EEXIST accepted, as before; Windows `_wstat64` + `_wmkdir`, no mode); (h−1)/17280+1. |
| `nodus_seg_build_t` (opaque), `int nodus_seg_build_open(const char *dir, uint64_t k, sqlite3 *ledger, nodus_seg_build_t **out)`, `void nodus_seg_build_free(nodus_seg_build_t *b)`, `void nodus_seg_build_discard(nodus_seg_build_t *b)`, `uint64_t nodus_seg_build_k(const nodus_seg_build_t *b)` | **NEW.** Open the build of k (refused when k is already HELD — its marker is never dropped); an invalid marker / stray `.idx` are removed, a markerless `.dat` goes back to `.dat.tmp`, an existing `.dat.tmp` is resumed (other k / count: restarted). free keeps the temporary, discard deletes it. `ledger` = the witness database (`SELECT block_id FROM v2_blocks`); since storage P-B1 (nodus 0.26.16) this is a thin wrapper over `nodus_seg_build_open_src` with `nodus_seg_hash_src_ledger(ledger)` — same signature, same behaviour (NULL ledger refused, nothing touched). Since P-B4 the build's file work (stray-file unlinks, `.dat` → `.dat.tmp`, `nodus_fs_open_rw_create` of `.dat.tmp`, size, truncate, positional writes; the resume scan's cuts) goes through `nodus_fs` — POSIX calls, flags and mode 0600 unchanged — and the build compiles for Windows. |
| `nodus_seg_hash_src_t { void *ctx; int (*hash_at)(void *ctx, uint64_t h, uint8_t out[64]); }`, `nodus_seg_hash_src_t nodus_seg_hash_src_ledger(sqlite3 *db)`, `int nodus_seg_build_open_src(const char *dir, uint64_t k, const nodus_seg_hash_src_t *src, nodus_seg_build_t **out)` | **NEW (storage P-B1, design `2026-10-19-windows-light-storage-design.md` §3 B1).** The builder's trusted block hashes come from a SOURCE: `hash_at` answers the canonical block hash at h — 0 found / 1 absent / -1 fault (exactly the old `ledger_hash` contract). The caller vouches that every 0-answer is the canonical hash; the builder re-checks every header, part, set and commit against it exactly as with the ledger (wrong hash at h+1 → `HEADER_HASH`, at h → `BLOCK_ID`; absent → `NO_LEDGER`; fault → `FAULT`; the resume scan returns -1 on either). The struct is copied; `ctx` is borrowed and must stay valid, giving the same answers, until the build is freed, discarded or finished. The ledger source runs today's `SELECT block_id FROM v2_blocks`. A light node passes a hash list it verified (design §3 B2): `nodus_light_hashlist_src` (storage P-B2, §24.12). Test: `test_storage_segment` case `hash_src` (byte-identical files through a non-ledger source; wrong / absent / fault answers behave like the ledger's). |
| `int nodus_seg_build_resume_step(nodus_seg_build_t *b, uint32_t max_heights)` | **NEW.** Re-verifies up to `max_heights` records of the partial file; cuts at the first short / torn / failing record; after the last height the commit and set records are re-verified from the file as ONE unit (the set's hash, then the commit's signatures against it — no state store involved): a short / torn / failing byte in either cuts at the start of the commit record, bytes after a good pair are cut. 1 over / 0 more / -1 fault (I/O, ledger read). |
| `int nodus_seg_build_next(const nodus_seg_build_t *b, uint64_t *h_out, uint32_t *part_out, bool *header_needed)` | **NEW.** What the build needs next (height, part, then `NODUS_SEG_PART_VALSET`, then `NODUS_SEG_PART_COMMIT`, whether header(h+1) is still missing): 0 / 1 complete / -1 scanning. (`nodus_seg_build_cur_header` had no caller and was deleted, dead-code pruning 2026-10-15.) |
| `nodus_seg_v_t nodus_seg_build_put_header(nodus_seg_build_t *b, uint64_t h, const uint8_t *hdr, size_t len)`, `nodus_seg_v_t nodus_seg_build_put_part_proto(nodus_seg_build_t *b, uint64_t h, uint32_t i, const uint8_t *proto, size_t len)`, `nodus_seg_v_t nodus_seg_build_put_part(nodus_seg_build_t *b, uint64_t h, uint32_t i, const uint8_t *part, size_t part_len, const uint8_t *proof, size_t proof_len)` | **NEW.** Add one verified piece (nothing is written unless it verifies): header(h+1) against v2_blocks[h+1] / [h]; a part (stored proto or wire form) against the header's part-set root. |
| `nodus_seg_v_t nodus_seg_build_put_valset(nodus_seg_build_t *b, const uint8_t *vs, size_t len)` | **NEW (K8a).** validators(k·P) as a cometbft ValidatorSet proto, after every height: 1 … `NODUS_SEG_VALSET_MAX` bytes, `cmt_pb_validator_set_unmarshal` + `cmt_validator_set_from_proto` (ValidateBasic) → `VALSET_DECODE`, `cmt_validator_set_hash` ≠ header(k·P).validators_hash → `COMMIT_VALSET`; held in the build, NOT written (a second set: `ORDER`). |
| `nodus_seg_v_t nodus_seg_build_put_commit(nodus_seg_build_t *b, const uint8_t *commit, size_t len)` | **CHANGED (K8a): the `nodus_cmt_store_t *state` parameter is removed.** Needs the held set (`ORDER` before it); decode, height k·P, block_id = header(k·P+1).last_block_id, the hash binding to header(k·P+1).last_commit_hash, then ALWAYS `cmt_verify_commit` against the held set (`COMMIT_SIGS`). On OK writes `clen ‖ commit ‖ vlen ‖ set` as one unit. |
| `int nodus_seg_build_finish(nodus_seg_build_t *b)` | **NEW.** Atomic publish: truncate + fsync data, write + fsync index, rename both, fsync dir, write + fsync marker, rename, fsync dir; frees the build. -1 = incomplete / I/O (build stays open). Since P-B4 the same order through `nodus_fs` (`nodus_fs_truncate`, `_fsync`, `_open_write_trunc` + `_pwrite_all`, `_rename`, `_dir_sync`; `nodus_witness_storage_segment.c:902`): POSIX calls unchanged; on Windows `_commit`, `MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING \| MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH)` and the directory flush (§24.11) — the `.dat.tmp` → `.dat` rename happens while the build's own descriptor is still open, which the Windows arm allows through `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`. |
| `bool nodus_seg_store_has(const nodus_cmt_store_t *store, uint64_t k)`, `int nodus_seg_export_step(nodus_seg_build_t *b, nodus_cmt_store_t *store, uint32_t max_heights, nodus_seg_v_t *why_out)` | **NEW; P-B4: MOVED to `nodus_witness_storage_segment_export.c` (Linux-only list), `nodus_seg_export_step` REWRITTEN over the build's public calls — signatures unchanged.** `store_has`: store base ≤ (k−1)·P+1 and height ≥ k·P+1 (`_export.c:48`, moved unchanged). `export_step` (`_export.c:75`): one bounded export pass (header from the meta of h+1, raw P:h:i, then validators(k·P) from the STATE table via `nodus_seg_valset_from_store`, then raw C:k·P) → 1 complete / 0 more / -1 (missing piece — including a state store that pruned validators(k·P), `*why_out` = OK, the holder then fetches the set — or a store that disagrees with the build's trusted hashes, `*why_out`). Since P-B4 it no longer reads the build's private fields: what to add next comes from `nodus_seg_build_next` (a build still in its resume scan, next = -1, → -1 as before when `b->scanning` was read), heights while `part < NODUS_SEG_PART_VALSET`, then the set when next is `NODUS_SEG_PART_VALSET`, then the commit when next is `NODUS_SEG_PART_COMMIT`; pieces go in only through `nodus_seg_build_put_header` / `_put_part_proto` / `_put_valset` / `_put_commit`, so each passes the same verification. Its scratch — the block meta, a `NODUS_SEG_HEADER_MAX` header buffer and a tail buffer of `EXPORT_TAIL_CAP` = `NODUS_SEG_COMMIT_MAX` bytes (`_export.c:46`) for the set and the commit (previously the build's own I/O scratch, same size) — is allocated at entry, so an out-of-memory step returns -1 before it touches the build (`_export.c:85-95`). It returns 1 only when `nodus_seg_build_next` itself answers 1 (complete); otherwise -1 with an ERROR log "export added every piece but the build is not complete" (`_export.c:179-186`, unreachable unless the build disagrees). The refusal log names the height being exported (`at_h`). |

OK / NOT_HELD / FAULT. |

### 24.9 Archive reward, K9 — the grace scales with the newly assigned segments; K6a — no fetch byte budget (decision `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-05-storage-reward-is-for-archive.md` K9, K6a; bytes doc `docs/plans/2026-10-05-archive-reward-bytes.md` item 4 K9 line; oracle `nodus/tests/vectors/archive_reward_kat.json` "grace_rule", reading 4)

Pre-activation (inert until the storage vote). Replaces G = 1. Rule: at storage boundary H, n(m) = the segments member m holds over set(H) (published ≤ H) that it did not hold over set(H−E) (published ≤ H−E) — **this count is replaced by K9a, §24.10**; n > 0 → grace_until = max(grace_until, H + n·E); epoch (H, H+E] is in grace for m iff H < grace_until (the value AFTER the update at H); in grace: no probe, weight 0, fail_streak unchanged.

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `int nodus_storage_grace_next(uint64_t prev, uint64_t H, uint64_t n, uint64_t E, uint64_t *out)` (`nodus_witness_v2_storage.h`) | **NEW (pure).** n == 0 → prev; else max(prev, H + n·E) with checked u64 (`dna_ck_mul_u64`, `dna_ck_add_u64`). 0 / -1 (overflow; the boundary also treats a result above INT64_MAX as a FAULT). Proven against the 36 oracle `grace_rule` vectors. |
| `int nodus_storage_in_grace(uint64_t H, uint64_t grace_until)` | **NEW (pure).** 1 iff H < grace_until. |
| `int nodus_storage_member_eligible(uint64_t H, uint64_t grace_until, int in_cur, int in_prev)` | **NEW (pure).** THE eligibility rule: 0 in grace, else 1 iff in_cur (m ∈ holders(k, set(H)), published ≤ H) or in_prev (m ∈ holders(k, set(H−E)), published ≤ H−E — the handoff overlap). |
| `static int st_member_walk(const nodus_storage_set_t *cur, const nodus_storage_set_t *prev, uint64_t H, const st_seg_t *segs, size_t n_segs, st_rel_fn rel, void *ud)`, `st_rel_fn` = `int (*)(void *ud, size_t member, uint64_t k, int in_cur, int in_prev)`, `st_rel_weight`, `st_rel_collect`, `st_rel_new` (`nodus_witness_v2_storage.c`) | **NEW (internal); replaces `st_eligible_walk` / `st_mark_weight` / `st_mark_collect` (REMOVED).** One walk over the published segments (k ascending) emitting each (member of set(H), k, in_cur, in_prev) with at least one flag set; the consumers: settlement weights and the eligible list (through `nodus_storage_member_eligible`) and the grace count (in_cur ∧ ¬in_prev). A holder of set(H) missing from set(H) is a fault; a holder of set(H−E) that left the set is skipped. |
| `static int st_grace_update(nodus_witness_t *w, uint64_t B, nodus_storage_set_t *set)`, `static int st_grace_write(nodus_witness_t *w, const uint8_t fp[64], uint64_t old, uint64_t nv)` | **NEW (internal).** Step 5a of the boundary (inside `st_freeze`, after publication): n per member over set(B−E) (absent = nothing held), the new grace_until written to `v2_storage_nodes` bound to the observed value and into the frozen copy; FAULT on overflow or a write that does not land. |

The scan (`storage_scan`, `nodus_witness_roots_v2.c`), `nodus_witness_storage_set_get`, `rtn_sys_stor_fetch`, `st_status_row` read it with typed checks. |
| `dnac_storage_status` `"gu"`, `nodus_dnac_storage_status_t.grace_until`, `nodus-cli storage status` | **CHANGED.** Live grace_until on the wire (row keys 7, map up to 15 keys); the CLI prints it and the in-grace state. |
| `nodus_witness_storage_holder.c` `serve_request` | **CHANGED (K6a, internal).** No budget check and no charge; the runtime struct lost its `budget[]` table. |

Written, not run by the builder. |

### 24.10 Archive reward, K9a — the grace counts only segments taken over (decision `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-05-storage-reward-is-for-archive.md` K9a, operator "tamam"; refines §24.9; no byte change; oracle `grace_rule` vectors unchanged — they take n as an input)

Pre-activation. Rule: at storage boundary H, n(m) = 0 for every member when storage_set(H−E) does not exist (the first storage boundary after activation); otherwise n(m) = the segments k with published ≤ H−E that m holds over set(H) and did not hold over set(H−E). A segment published in (H−E, H] — at H itself included — is not counted; an existing EMPTY set(H−E) is a set. The update (`nodus_storage_grace_next`), the in-grace test, the one eligibility rule (the overlap included), the must-hold list (no grace gate) and the frozen copy (the value AFTER the update) are unchanged.

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `int nodus_storage_grace_counts(uint64_t H, uint64_t E, uint64_t published_height, int prev_exists, int in_cur, int in_prev)` (`nodus_witness_v2_storage.h`) | **NEW (pure).** 1 iff `prev_exists` and H ≥ E and published_height ≤ H − E and in_cur and not in_prev; else 0 (H < E checked before the subtraction). The only predicate the freeze's n(m) goes through. |
| `st_rel_fn` = `int (*)(void *ud, size_t member, uint64_t k, uint64_t pub, int in_cur, int in_prev)`; `st_rel_weight`, `st_rel_collect`, `st_rel_new` (`nodus_witness_v2_storage.c`) | **CHANGED (internal).** `st_member_walk` now hands each segment's published_height (`pub`) to the consumer; weight and collect ignore it; `st_rel_new` counts through `nodus_storage_grace_counts` (`st_newcount_t` gains `H` and `prev_exists`). |
| `static int st_grace_update(nodus_witness_t *w, uint64_t B, nodus_storage_set_t *set)` | **CHANGED (behaviour, internal).** `prev_exists` = set(B−E) read back as a frozen set (`nodus_witness_storage_set_get` 0, count 0 included); absent (1) → n = 0 for everyone. Signature unchanged. |
| Tests | `test_storage_b2` section D (K9a model: n = 0 without set(B−E), only segments published ≤ B−E; boundary E — no grace, every member's eligible list = its holdings over set(E), H = E settles all OK and pays; 2E — X's registration takeover gives 2E + n·E with n > 0; the skip gainer and the exit unchanged) and new case `grace_count_k9a` (section D1, the predicate pure: published at H / H−1 / H−E+1 → 0, at H−E → 1, no set(H−E) → 0, H < E → 0). The engine run cannot publish a segment (k = 9 due at 9·P + 2E blocks), so "a newly published segment gives no grace" is proven by D1 only. Written, not run by the builder. |

### 24.11 Storage P-B4 — the portable file layer `nodus_fs` and the split of the segment export (nodus 0.26.16; `nodus/src/storage/nodus_fs.{h,c}` NEW, `nodus/src/witness/nodus_witness_storage_segment_export.c` NEW; governing: `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-19-kurultay-26b-summary.md` item 9, `2026-10-16-kurultay-25-summary.md` K25-5 / K25-6 / K25-7; intent: design `docs/plans/2026-10-19-windows-light-storage-design.md` §3 B4 and "Rev 1 corrections")

A small FILE layer for the segment files so the segment builder and reader compile for Windows. On Linux / Android every call except two is ONE POSIX call — the call, flags and mode the segment code issued before this layer existed — and its Windows counterpart on Windows; `nodus_fs_is_dir` is a composition (stat, then `S_ISDIR`) and `nodus_fs_mkdir_p` is new (one `nodus_fs_mkdir` per level) (`nodus_fs.h:7-14`). The layer decides nothing: callers keep every rule (what to open, rename order, when a short read is a torn record). In the COMMON source list (`nodus/CMakeLists.txt:149`). Callers today: `nodus_witness_storage_segment.c` and the Windows test `test_seg_file_win`. Node-local: nothing here reads a clock, draws randomness or touches a consensus value (`nodus_fs.h:53-54`).

**Handles, paths, offsets, errors** (`nodus_fs.h:16-51`, `nodus_fs.c:100-153`, `:262-374`):
- **Handles** are C runtime file descriptors (`int`) on both platforms. On Windows the descriptor wraps a `HANDLE` from `CreateFileW` with `FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE | FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL`, NULL security attributes (not inheritable), handed to `_open_osfhandle` (`_O_BINARY` with the access mode). `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` is what lets the segment build rename `.dat.tmp` → `.dat` (and discard / unlink `.dat.tmp`) while its own descriptor is still open — the POSIX order, kept. If `_open_osfhandle` fails the HANDLE is closed by this layer and errno is the CRT's code or `EMFILE` (the CRT's ownership on failure is NOT verified — no UCRT source in the tree; `nodus_fs.c:351-358`).
- **Paths are UTF-8** on both platforms. On Windows every path call (open, rename, unlink, stat, mkdir, dir sync) converts with `MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8, MB_ERR_INVALID_CHARS)` into a buffer of `FS_WPATH_MAX` = 1024 UTF-16 units and calls the wide API, so a writer and a reader always name the same file. Not valid UTF-8 (or a NULL path) → -1, errno `EINVAL`; does not fit 1024 → -1, errno `ENAMETOOLONG`. No `\\?\` long-path prefix is added: a path longer than the Windows limit fails.
- **64-bit offsets.** `nodus_fs.c` defines `_FILE_OFFSET_BITS 64` before any include on every non-Windows build (`:112-114`) and refuses to compile if `off_t` is not 8 bytes (`_Static_assert(sizeof(off_t) == 8, "nodus_fs needs a 64-bit off_t")`, `:153`). Reason: a 32-bit ABI — Android armeabi-v7a, which builds this file through messenger's `add_subdirectory(nodus)` — would otherwise truncate every offset ≥ 2 GiB in the `(off_t)` casts; the segment format addresses `.dat` with u64 offsets. On 64-bit Linux the instructions are the same, only the libc symbol names change (`open` → `open64`, `pread` → `pread64`, …, aliases of one entry point). The Windows arm uses 64-bit offsets directly (`OVERLAPPED.Offset` / `OffsetHigh`, `_fstat64`, `_wstat64`, `_chsize_s`).
- **Errors: -1 with errno set.** POSIX: the call's own errno, untouched. Windows, Win32 code → errno in `win_errno` (`nodus_fs.c:280-317`): `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` / `ERROR_PATH_NOT_FOUND` → `ENOENT`; `ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS` / `ERROR_FILE_EXISTS` → `EEXIST`; `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` / `ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION` / `ERROR_LOCK_VIOLATION` → `EACCES`; `ERROR_DELETE_PENDING` → `EBUSY` (a name whose delete is pending; it may also surface as `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` → `EACCES` — the two cannot be told apart without the NTSTATUS, which this layer does not read; this reading is not verified); `ERROR_FILENAME_EXCED_RANGE` → `ENAMETOOLONG`; `ERROR_DISK_FULL` / `ERROR_HANDLE_DISK_FULL` → `ENOSPC`; anything else → `EIO`. Every code except the ENOENT / EEXIST ones is logged at DEBUG (tag `NODUS_FS`) with its number BEFORE errno is set (the logger cannot overwrite it). Other Windows errnos: `EBADF` (a descriptor without a handle, `_get_osfhandle` = -1), `EIO` (a 0-byte `WriteFile`, a negative `_fstat64` size), `EINVAL` (truncate length > `INT64_MAX`). The calls that go through the CRT — `_wunlink`, `_wmkdir`, `_wstat64`, `_commit`, `_fstat64`, `_chsize_s` — carry the CRT's OWN errno mapping, NOT verified (no CRT source in the tree). Callers that test `errno == ENOENT` / `errno == EEXIST` behave the same on both platforms: those two only ever mean "not found" / "already exists".
- **Windows API behaviour is this file's reading** — none of it has run on native Windows, and none of it is checked against Microsoft's documentation (no copy in the tree; `nodus_fs.c:11-13`). Under Wine 8.0 it ran in `test_seg_file_win` (nodus `README.md` 0.26.16 row).

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `int nodus_fs_open_write_trunc(const char *path)` | **NEW.** Open for writing, created when absent, TRUNCATED when present. POSIX `open(path, O_WRONLY \| O_CREAT \| O_TRUNC, 0600)`; Windows `CreateFileW(GENERIC_WRITE, CREATE_ALWAYS)` — no POSIX mode, the file takes its directory's ACL. fd / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_open_rw_create(const char *path)` | **NEW.** Read + write, created when absent, NOT truncated. POSIX `open(path, O_RDWR \| O_CREAT, 0600)`; Windows `CreateFileW(GENERIC_READ \| GENERIC_WRITE, OPEN_ALWAYS)`. fd / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_open_read(const char *path)` | **NEW.** An existing file, read-only. POSIX `open(path, O_RDONLY)`; Windows `CreateFileW(GENERIC_READ, OPEN_EXISTING)`. fd / -1, errno `ENOENT` when the file does not exist — the segment reader's "not held" (1) versus "fault" (-1) rests on it. |
| `int nodus_fs_close(int fd)` | **NEW.** POSIX `close`; Windows `_close` (closes the HANDLE). 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_pwrite_all(int fd, const uint8_t *p, size_t n, uint64_t off)` | **NEW.** All `n` bytes at the 64-bit offset `off`, never through a shared file position. POSIX: `pwrite` until done, `EINTR` retried, a 0-byte write is -1 (the segment code's former `pwrite_all`, moved unchanged). Windows: `WriteFile` with an `OVERLAPPED` offset, chunks of at most `FS_CHUNK_MAX` = 1 GiB (`(size_t)1 << 30`, a DWORD count); the handle is synchronous, the call moves the handle's file pointer as a side effect, which nothing reads. 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_pread_all(int fd, uint8_t *p, size_t n, uint64_t off)` | **NEW.** All `n` bytes at `off`. POSIX: `pread` until done, `EINTR` retried (the former `pread_all`). Windows: `ReadFile` with an `OVERLAPPED` offset, chunks ≤ 1 GiB; end of file is accepted in BOTH forms (FALSE with `ERROR_HANDLE_EOF`, or TRUE with 0 bytes — which one Windows gives is not verified). 0 all read / 1 end of file first / -1. |
| `int64_t nodus_fs_read_once(int fd, uint8_t *p, size_t n)` | **NEW.** ONE read at the descriptor's current position (a fresh descriptor: offset 0) — not a loop: the caller sizes `n` to see a file longer than it should be (the marker check reads `NODUS_SEG_DONE_LEN + 1`). POSIX one `read()`; Windows one `ReadFile` without `OVERLAPPED` (≤ 1 GiB; `ERROR_HANDLE_EOF` → 0). Bytes read (0 at end of file) / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_fsync(int fd)` | **NEW.** POSIX `fsync`; Windows `_commit` (the CRT's `FlushFileBuffers`, as `nodus_p2p_host.c`'s atomic replace). 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_size(int fd, uint64_t *size_out)` | **NEW.** POSIX `fstat`; Windows `_fstat64` (a negative size → -1, `EIO`). 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_truncate(int fd, uint64_t len)` | **NEW.** Cut (or extend with zeros) to `len`. POSIX `ftruncate`; Windows `_chsize_s` (`len` > `INT64_MAX` → -1, `EINVAL`; a non-zero `errno_t` is copied to errno). 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_dir_sync(const char *dir)` | **NEW.** Make the directory's entries (renames, new files, unlinks) durable. POSIX `open(dir, O_RDONLY \| O_DIRECTORY)`, `fsync`, `close` (the former `fsync_dir`). Windows: `CreateFileW(GENERIC_WRITE, OPEN_EXISTING, FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS)` on the directory, `FlushFileBuffers`, `CloseHandle` — an open or flush failure is -1 with errno, never a silent 0. What NTFS makes durable through that flush is NOT verified, and under Wine the flush may be a no-op; `nodus_fs_rename` is `WRITE_THROUGH` on top. If the entries are not durable, after a crash an unlinked segment file may reappear: a complete set (.ok, .idx, .dat) is the segment exactly as published; a partial one fails the reader's marker / size / index-hash checks and is not held (`nodus_fs.c:74-85`). 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_rename(const char *from, const char *to)` | **NEW.** Rename, replacing `to` when it exists, in one step. POSIX `rename`; Windows `MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING \| MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH)` (as `nodus_p2p_host.c`; `WRITE_THROUGH` returns only once the move is on disk). A rename onto a target ANOTHER descriptor holds open stays one `MoveFileExW`; where Windows refuses it the caller gets -1 (`test_seg_file_win` case 10 prints which). No caller needs more: the segment build renames onto names it has just unlinked or never created (`nodus_fs.c:40-48`). 0 / -1. |
| `#define NODUS_FS_ASIDE_TAG ".deleted."` | **NEW.** The tag of the aside name the Windows unlink moves a file to: `"<path>" NODUS_FS_ASIDE_TAG "<pid>.<n>"`. Never a segment file name: `nodus_seg_name_parse` accepts only `seg-<k>` followed by EXACTLY one of its six suffixes, so `seg-<k>.dat.deleted.…` is not read as a segment (`nodus_fs.h:133-137`). |
| `int nodus_fs_unlink_if_exists(const char *path)` | **NEW.** Remove `path`; absent is not an error. POSIX `unlink`, `ENOENT` accepted (the segment code's former `unlink` call). Windows — "free the NAME first" (P-B4-FIX2, `nodus_fs.c:535-592`): a directory (`GetFileAttributesW`) goes straight to `_wunlink` (refused, as before); otherwise `MoveFileExW(path → "<path>.deleted.<GetCurrentProcessId()>.<n>", 0)` — no replace, `<n>` a process-wide `InterlockedIncrement` counter, in the same directory — then `DeleteFileW` on the aside name; returns 0 once the move succeeded, whether or not the delete did (a still-held file is delete-pending under the aside name and goes at its last close). So the name can be created again at once, as a NEW file, while a holder keeps reading the old bytes — the POSIX answer. Why: a file unlinked while another handle holds it becomes delete-pending UNDER ITS NAME until the last close; under Wine 8.0 a re-create even reached the held file (observed by the ORCHESTRATOR, `test_seg_file_win` case 10). Source absent → 0 (`ENOENT`); an aside name already taken (a crash leftover of the same pid) → the next counter, at most `FS_ASIDE_TRIES` = 8 times; any other move failure, an aside name that does not fit (`plen + 64` bytes, or `FS_WPATH_MAX`), or an allocation failure → the previous direct `_wunlink` (`ENOENT` accepted, CRT errno). One answer differs from `_wunlink` (this file's reading, not verified): a READ-ONLY file was refused (-1) and is now moved aside, its delete refused, 0 returned with the aside file left — this layer never creates one (`FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL`). The aside name is logged at DEBUG only. A crash between the move and the last close (or a refused delete) can leave `*.deleted.*` files; nothing reads them and no startup cleanup removes them. 0 removed or absent / -1 (errno). |
| `int nodus_fs_exists(const char *path)` | **NEW.** POSIX `stat`; Windows `_wstat64` (a path that does not convert → 0). 1 / 0. |
| `int nodus_fs_is_dir(const char *path)` | **NEW (composition).** POSIX `stat` + `S_ISDIR`; Windows `_wstat64` + `_S_IFDIR`. 1 a directory / 0 exists, not a directory / -1 stat failed (errno). |
| `int nodus_fs_mkdir(const char *path)` | **NEW.** ONE level. POSIX `mkdir(path, 0700)`; Windows `_wmkdir` (no mode: the parent's ACL). An existing entry → -1, errno `EEXIST`, as `mkdir`. 0 / -1. |
| `int nodus_fs_mkdir_p(const char *path)` | **NEW (no equivalent before this layer; `nodus_fs.c:623-661`, shared by both arms).** `path` and every missing parent (`nodus_fs_mkdir` per level; a parent-level failure is not decisive); the last level must end up a directory (`EEXIST` accepted, then `nodus_fs_is_dir` must say 1, 0 → `ENOTDIR`). Separators `'/'` — on Windows also `'\\'`; a Windows drive prefix (`"C:"`) and a leading root are skipped. Empty / NULL → `EINVAL`, allocation failure → `ENOMEM`. Used today only by `test_seg_file_win`. 0 / -1. |
| `static void win_errno(DWORD e, const char *what, const char *path)`, `static int widen(const char *s, wchar_t out[FS_WPATH_MAX])`, `static int win_open(const char *path, DWORD access, DWORD disposition, int crt_flags)`, `static HANDLE fd_handle(int fd)`, `static int unlink_direct(const wchar_t *w)`; `FS_WPATH_MAX` (1024), `FS_CHUNK_MAX` (1 GiB), `FS_ASIDE_TRIES` (8), `FS_IS_SEP(c)` (`'/'` on POSIX, `'/'` or `'\\'` on Windows) (`nodus_fs.c`, Windows arm except `FS_IS_SEP`) | **NEW (internal).** The Win32 → errno map, the UTF-8 → UTF-16 conversion, the shared `CreateFileW` + `_open_osfhandle` open, the descriptor → HANDLE lookup, the direct `_wunlink` fallback. |
| Segment file helpers (`nodus_witness_storage_segment.c`) | **CHANGED (internal).** `static int pwrite_all`, `static int pread_all`, `static int fsync_dir`, `static int file_exists` REMOVED (now `nodus_fs_pwrite_all`, `_pread_all`, `_dir_sync`, `_exists`); `write_file_sync` and `unlink_quiet` kept, written over `nodus_fs` (`unlink_quiet` still logs a WARN with `strerror(errno)` on failure). The file no longer includes `<fcntl.h>`, `<sys/stat.h>`, `<unistd.h>`. |
| `nodus_witness_storage_segment_export.c`: `static int store_raw(nodus_cmt_store_t *s, const char *key, const uint8_t **v, size_t *n)`, `static int on_height(const nodus_seg_build_t *b, uint64_t *h, uint32_t *part, bool *hdr_needed)`, `EXPORT_TAIL_CAP` | **NEW file (internal helpers).** `store_raw` moved unchanged (0 found / 1 absent / -1); `on_height` = `nodus_seg_build_next(...) == 0 && *part < NODUS_SEG_PART_VALSET` (the build is still on a height); `EXPORT_TAIL_CAP` = `(size_t)NODUS_SEG_COMMIT_MAX`. The four public functions it holds: §24.8 rows. |
| Tests | `test_seg_file_win` (`nodus/tests/test_seg_file_win.c`, NEW, Windows-only executable, no ctest entry; `nodus/CMakeLists.txt:1292-1297` as of storage P-B2): nodus_fs on Windows (positional I/O across 2^31 and — sparse file — 2^32, truncate, rename-replace also of an open source, unlink of an open file, mkdir / mkdir -p, UTF-8 names, `ENOENT`, directory sync, the open-target / pending-unlink orders, aside names refused by `nodus_seg_name_parse`) and, with a Linux fixture, the segment reader, an interrupted build + republish, and a torn record — byte-identical to the fixture. Fixture: `NODUS_SEG_WIN_FIXTURE=<dir> ./test_storage_segment` (case `win_fixture`). Exit 0 / 1 failed / 2 something did not run (no fixture, or case 11 NOT COVERED) — 2 is NOT a pass. Requirements, what it leaves behind and how it can lie (Wine ≠ Windows): nodus `README.md` row 0.26.16 and the file header. |

### 24.12 Storage P-B2 — the light hash list: one historical segment's trusted block hashes without a ledger (`nodus/src/storage/nodus_light_hashlist.{h,c}` NEW, `nodus/src/witness/nodus_witness_storage_fetch_wire.c` NEW; branch `stor-b2`, not versioned; governing: `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-19-kurultay-26b-summary.md` item 9 — piece B is the historical-segment builder only, serving is a later package —, `2026-10-16-kurultay-25-summary.md` K25-9 one light client / K25-12 trust root = the pinned nodes; intent: design `docs/plans/2026-10-19-windows-light-storage-design.md` §3 B2 and its "Rev 1 corrections" — the B2 verification contract and cost —, §5, §6 G1 / G4)

A light node has no `v2_blocks`; the segment build (§24.8) needs the trusted hash at every height of [first, last + 1] of segment k (first = (k−1)·P + 1, last = k·P). This list builds those P + 1 hashes from ONE light-verified header by hash chaining backwards, executing no block and checking no signature per height: the anchor header(k·P + 1) gives hash(last + 1) = its own hash and hash(last) = its `last_block_id.hash`; then for h = last − 1 down to first ONE channel 0x73 request `(k, h, part 0, cont 0)` — the existing wire, whose answer carries header(h + 1) and part 0 of block h (`nodus_witness_storage_fetch.h:45-66`; the part is dropped, design §3 B3) — and header(h + 1) is accepted only after, in this order (`nodus_light_hashlist.c:188-230`): (a) the answer decodes, answers THIS request (same rq), is OK, has the cont-0 shape; (b) the header decodes; (c) its height is exactly h + 1 (`cmt_light_verify_backwards` does not check heights, `cmt_light_verifier.h:361`); (d) `cmt_light_verify_backwards(candidate, the trusted header above, block_protocol)` — the reference's VerifyBackwards (ValidateBasic, same chain id, strictly earlier time, hash = the trusted header's `last_block_id.hash`); (e) `cmt_header_hash(candidate)` equals the hash the list holds for h + 1 (checked by this file, the same equality as (d)'s last clause); (f) its `last_block_id.hash` is 64 bytes. Then hash(h) = that `last_block_id.hash`. P − 1 requests per segment (17 279 at P = 17 280). Rotation — the holder's rule (`nodus_witness_storage_holder.h` "THE FETCH CLIENT"): one request outstanding to one peer; silence, a refusal, a malformed answer or a failed check → the next peer (index + 1 mod n); an accepted header keeps the peer; a full round of n peers without an accepted header → `NODUS_LHL_STEP_ROUND` (the caller waits and steps again). The transport is the caller's callback: no socket, no clock, no file, no sleep here. Node-local: no consensus value is read, written or computed. Heap: (P + 1) × 64 bytes + two decoded headers + one `NODUS_STFETCH_MSG_MAX` answer buffer. Compiles for Windows: both files are in the COMMON source list (`nodus/CMakeLists.txt:359`, `:365`); the serving side `nodus_witness_storage_fetch.c` stays Linux-only (`:766`). No caller yet (serving / the assignment wait for HF-7, K25-7); no `nodus-light` command (nodus-light speaks 4001 only and has no 4004 host).

| Function | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| `typedef int (*nodus_light_fetch_fn)(void *ctx, size_t peer, const uint8_t *req, size_t req_len, uint8_t *ans, size_t cap, size_t *ans_len)`, `nodus_light_fetch_t { void *ctx; size_t n_peers; nodus_light_fetch_fn request; }` | **NEW.** The caller's 0x73 transport: send the 38-byte REQUEST message to peer `peer` (0 … n_peers − 1), wait for that peer's answer (the caller's timeout). 0 an answer in `ans[0 .. *ans_len)` (any bytes — every check is the list's) / 1 no answer (silence, timeout, not connected, larger than `cap`) / -1 a local fault. |
| `nodus_lhl_refusal_t` (`NODUS_LHL_R_SILENT`, `_ANSWER`, `_RQ`, `_REFUSED`, `_SHAPE`, `_HDR_DECODE`, `_HEIGHT`, `_BACKWARDS`, `_HASH`, `_BLOCK_ID`, `_COUNT`), `const char *nodus_lhl_refusal_str(nodus_lhl_refusal_t r)`, `nodus_lhl_stats_t { requests; accepted; refused[NODUS_LHL_R_COUNT]; }` | **NEW.** Why a candidate was not accepted (checks a–f above, and silence); a short log name (never NULL); the counters. |
| `nodus_light_hashlist_t` (opaque), `int nodus_light_hashlist_new(uint64_t k, const cmt_header_t *anchor, uint64_t block_protocol, nodus_light_hashlist_t **out)`, `void nodus_light_hashlist_free(nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl)`, `uint64_t nodus_light_hashlist_k(const nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl)` | **NEW.** A list for k anchored at `anchor` = header(k·P + 1), which the CALLER vouches the light client verified (G4). Copied. -1: k = 0, k·P + 1 outside int64 (`nodus_stfetch_req_valid`'s rule), NULL, an anchor height other than exactly k·P + 1 (WARN), an anchor whose own hash is nil or whose `last_block_id.hash` is not 64 bytes, out of memory. `free` zeroes and frees the list (NULL-safe). |
| `int nodus_light_hashlist_new_from_client(cmt_light_client_t *c, uint64_t k, cmt_time_t now, uint64_t block_protocol, nodus_light_hashlist_t **out, cmt_light_client_error_t *err)` | **NEW.** The anchor from the ONE light client (K25-9): `cmt_light_client_verify_light_block_at_height(c, k·P + 1, now, …)` — a block it already trusts as is, else fetched from its primary, verified and saved (the light client's rules unchanged) — then `nodus_light_hashlist_new`. 0 / 1 the light client refused that height (`*err`, WARN) / -1 fault or k refused. Not driven by a test (the light client is `test_cmt_light_client`'s subject). |
| `int nodus_light_hashlist_step(nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl, const nodus_light_fetch_t *f, uint32_t max_headers)`; `NODUS_LHL_STEP_MORE` 0, `_DONE` 1, `_ROUND` 2, `_FAULT` -1 | **NEW.** The header pass, at most `max_headers` accepted headers per call (rotation above). DONE once header(first + 1) is accepted (INFO: hashes held, requests, refused) and on every later call; ROUND after n consecutive refusals (WARN; the counter restarts, the next call starts at the next peer); FAULT on NULL / no peers / a transport -1 / a hash backend fault. A refusal is logged at DEBUG with its reason. A peer index ≥ n (the list shrank) restarts at 0. |
| `uint64_t nodus_light_hashlist_next_height(const nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl)`, `size_t nodus_light_hashlist_peer(const nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl)`, `nodus_lhl_stats_t nodus_light_hashlist_stats(const nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl)` | **NEW.** The h of the next request (its answer brings header(h + 1)), 0 when the pass is over; the next peer; the counters (copied). |
| `nodus_seg_hash_src_t nodus_light_hashlist_src(const nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl)` | **NEW.** The list as the build's hash source (§24.8 P-B1 row): `hash_at(h)` = 0 with the hash for h in [first, last + 1] ONLY once the pass is over, 1 (absent) for any other h and for every h before that, -1 for a NULL ctx. Points at `hl` (borrowed; must outlive every build over it). A complete list never changes, so it answers the same hashes for as long as it lives. |
| `static bool k_ok(uint64_t k)`, `static int exchange(nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl, const nodus_light_fetch_t *f, nodus_lhl_refusal_t *why)`, `static bool pass_over(nodus_light_hashlist_t *hl)`, `static int lhl_hash_at(void *ctx, uint64_t h, uint8_t out[64])`; `LHL_P` | **NEW (internal).** The k rule (a part-0 request at k·P must be valid); one exchange and checks a–f (1 accepted / 0 refused / -1 fault; nothing is stored unless every check passed); the end-of-pass test; the source's callback; `DNA_V2_SEGMENT_BLOCKS`. |
| Tests | `test_storage_segment` case `light_hashlist` (written, not run by the builder): the anchor header(P+1) from the fixture; honest pass = P − 1 requests, bounded steps, unfinished list answers absent, finished list == `v2_blocks.block_id` at 1 … P+1; a segment built over the list, its pieces fetched as 0x73 answers, publishes `.dat` / `.idx` / `.ok` byte-identical to the full node's export; ten kinds of bad first answer (top and bottom request included) each refused for its own reason and the other peer asked; one lying peer → ROUND, no progress; three bad peers → each asked once, ROUND; an honest peer restores progress; a transport fault → FAULT; constructor refusals. How it can lie: the anchor is not light-verified there; reasons HASH and BLOCK_ID are never reached (VerifyBackwards refuses every changed header first); no real 4004 transport. |

## 25. Light client packages 1-3 — cometbft @v0.38.26 `light/` (package 1: `shared/dnac/cmt_light_verifier.h`, `cmt_light_block.h`, `cmt_light_store.h`, `cmt_light_provider.h`, `cmt_fraction.h`; additions to `cmt_time.h`, `cmt_validation.h`, `cmt_validator_set.h` — package 2: `cmt_light_client.h` §25.9 — package 3: `cmt_light_detector.h` §25.10)

No other function-reference file covers the `shared/dnac/cmt_*` consensus port, so its light-client entries are kept here, next to the version-3 chain's other nodus-side entries. Why: decision `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-16-relay-port-4003-bandwidth-role.md` (B9 — the bandwidth and storage roles become light clients, no block execution, so that they run on Windows); order and store rules: `docs/plans/decisions/2026-10-16-kurultay-25-summary.md` items 1, 2, 7, 8. Reference pin: `docs/plans/decisions/2026-09-30-cometbft-pin-v0.38.26.md`.

**What this is and is not.** Package 1 (§25.1-§25.8): the verifier, the LightBlock type + codec, the store and the provider INTERFACE. Packages 2 and 3 (§25.9 client, §25.10 detector) and the pinned networked provider over the `dnac_light_block` RPC (§26) were added after it. There is still no proof layer, so nothing here may be called a "reference-equivalent light client" or "proven RPC" (Kurultay #25 standing conditions; item 6: "proven RPC" waits for HF-7 / HF-8 and a tested proof API per method). **No production caller** for the light-client entries (§25.1, §25.3-§25.10, and the provider of §26): no node or client path calls them (`cmt_light_client.h:8-11`, `cmt_light_detector.h:10-17`, `nodus_light_provider.h:8-14`); their callers are `test_cmt_light_verifier`, `test_cmt_light_block`, `test_cmt_light_store`, `test_cmt_light_client`, `test_cmt_light_detector` and `test_light_block_rpc`, and — since nodus 0.26.11 — the separate program `nodus-light` (`nodus/tools/nodus-light.c`: `cmt_light_store_db_open` `:753`, `nodus_light_provider_init` `:636`, `cmt_light_client_new` `:1019`, `cmt_light_client_update` `:1076`, `cmt_light_client_attack_clear` `:1351`), which is not a node path (the source headers still read "NO PRODUCTION CALLER"). The exception is §25.2: `cmt_time_after` / `cmt_time_add_ns` are also called by `cmt_cs.c` (the live consensus vote-time clamp). All `.c` files are in nodus's common source list (`nodus/CMakeLists.txt`, `NODUS_SOURCES`, next to `cmt_validation.c`), outside the Linux-only list, so a Windows build compiles them. No function here reads a clock: `now` and every duration (int64 nanoseconds, Go `time.Duration`) are parameters. Return contract: `CMT_OK` / `CMT_REJECT` / `CMT_FAULT` (`cmt_tmhash.h`). Narrative: `nodus/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Light client package 1".

### 25.1 `cmt_fraction.h` (`libs/math/fraction.go:11-18`)

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `typedef struct { uint64_t numerator; uint64_t denominator; } cmt_fraction_t;` | **NEW.** `cmtmath.Fraction`, the type only. Not range-checked by the type; the functions that take one check what they need (a zero denominator in the Trusting family; [1/3, 1] in `cmt_light_validate_trust_level`). `ParseFraction` / `String` not ported. |

### 25.2 `cmt_time.h` — time helpers made public

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `bool cmt_time_after(cmt_time_t a, cmt_time_t b)` | **NEW (public; moved).** Go `Time.After`, strictly later: compares seconds, then nanos. Body unchanged from `cmt_cs.c`'s static `cs_time_after`. Callers: `cmt_cs.c` (state.go:2424, the vote-time clamp) and `cmt_light_verifier.c`. |
| `bool cmt_time_before(cmt_time_t a, cmt_time_t b)` | **NEW.** Go `Time.Before`, strictly earlier: `cmt_time_after(b, a)`; equal instants are neither. Caller: `cmt_light_verifier.c`. |
| `cmt_time_t cmt_time_add_ns(cmt_time_t t, int64_t ns)` | **NEW (public; moved).** Go `t.Add(d)` for a nanosecond duration, nanos carried into [0, 1e9), formed in unsigned words (Go's int64 wraps; C signed overflow is undefined). Body unchanged from `cmt_cs.c`'s static `cs_time_add_ns`. Callers: `cmt_cs.c` (state.go:2419, :2421) and `cmt_light_verifier.c`. |
| `static bool cs_time_after(...)`, `static cmt_time_t cs_time_add_ns(...)` (`cmt_cs.c`) | **REMOVED (internal)** — replaced by the two public functions above; `cmt_cs_vote_time` now calls them. Consensus behaviour unchanged. |

### 25.3 `cmt_validation.h` / `cmt_validator_set.h` — the Trusting family (`types/validation.go:118-193`, `types/validator_set.go:777-797`)

| Item | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `int cmt_verify_commit_light_trusting(const uint8_t *chain_id, size_t chain_id_len, cmt_validator_set_t *vals, const cmt_commit_t *commit, cmt_fraction_t trust_level, cmt_vs_error_t *err)` | **NEW.** `VerifyCommitLightTrusting` = `verifyCommitLightTrustingInternal(..., false)`. Unlike the `VerifyCommitLight` pair: no `verifyBasicValsAndCommit` (the set need not be the commit's); threshold `TotalVotingPower * Numerator / Denominator`, a zero Denominator refused first, `safeMul` overflow refused, comparison STRICT (`got <= needed` rejects); signers found BY ADDRESS (`look_up_by_index = false`: a non-member's signature skipped, the same validator twice refused); `trust_level` NOT range-checked. Early exit once the trust level is reached. `vals` not const (TotalVotingPower writes its cache). `err` may be NULL. CMT_OK; CMT_REJECT (zero Denominator, overflow, any `cmt_verify_commit_single` rejection); CMT_FAULT on NULL set or commit (R1B-10). |

CMT_OK / CMT_REJECT / CMT_FAULT. |

CMT_OK / CMT_REJECT / CMT_FAULT. |
| `int cmt_validator_set_verify_commit_light_trusting_all_signatures(cmt_validator_set_t *vals, const uint8_t *chain_id, size_t chain_id_len, const cmt_pb_commit_t *commit, cmt_fraction_t trust_level, cmt_vs_error_t *err)` | **NEW.** Method form (validator_set.go:788-797); delegates to `cmt_verify_commit_light_trusting_all_signatures`. |
| `CMT_SIG_POLICY_LIGHT` (`cmt_commit_sig_policy_t`), `int cmt_verify_commit_single(...)` | **CHANGED (callers only; signatures unchanged).** The Trusting pair passes `CMT_SIG_POLICY_LIGHT` (validation.go:177, :180) and `look_up_by_index = false` (:187, :192) — the address branch of `verifyCommitSingle`, until now called only from `test_cmt_validation.c`, now has library callers. |

No signature checked. `block_protocol` = the value Version.Block must equal (normally `CMT_BLOCK_PROTOCOL`). CMT_OK / CMT_REJECT; CMT_FAULT on NULL `sh`, NULL `chain_id` with non-zero length, or a hash backend failure. |

OLD_HEADER_EXPIRED: `at` (= trusted time + trusting period) and `now`. NEW_VAL_SET_CANT_BE_TRUSTED: `reason` = ErrNotEnoughVotingPowerSigned {got, needed}. INVALID_HEADER: `reason` carries ErrNotEnoughVotingPowerSigned when that was the cause, else `reason.code` = `CMT_VS_ERR_NONE`. NONE on CMT_REJECT: the adjacency checks, the NextValidatorsHash mismatch, a Trusting rejection that is not a power shortfall, ValidateTrustLevel. No error text. |

Both sets not const (TotalVotingPower cache). Allocates and frees the set-hash leaf scratch. CMT_OK / CMT_REJECT; CMT_FAULT on a NULL argument, a NULL embedded header in either signed header, or an allocation / backend failure. `err` may be NULL. |

No `trusted_vals` parameter, as in the reference. CMT_OK / CMT_REJECT / CMT_FAULT as for non-adjacent. |

Returns what the chosen function returns; CMT_FAULT on a NULL header. Does not call `cmt_light_validate_trust_level`. |

CMT_OK; CMT_FAULT on NULL `h`, `h->header` or `out`. |

The height relation is NOT checked, by the reference either. CMT_OK / CMT_REJECT; CMT_FAULT on a NULL header or a hash backend failure. |

CMT_OK; CMT_REJECT on malformed / over-capacity bytes or an invalid member; CMT_FAULT on NULL or allocation failure. |

### 25.6 `cmt_light_store.h` (`light/store/store.go:5-48`, `light/store/errors.go`, `light/store/db/db.go`)

One SQLite file per store (tables `cmt_light_block` (prefix, height, value; PRIMARY KEY (prefix, height)), `cmt_light_size` (prefix PRIMARY KEY, size) and — PROJECT, D9 — `cmt_light_attack` (prefix BLOB, seq INTEGER, cleared INTEGER, value BLOB; PRIMARY KEY (prefix, seq)), `cmt_light_store.c:53-59`); the value is the marshalled LightBlock (`cmt_light_block_encode`). Every write is `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` … `COMMIT` with `PRAGMA synchronous=FULL`; a COMMIT that fails is rolled back and the call returns CMT_FAULT — no retry, no busy timeout (commit `269f964f2`); the in-memory count changes only after a successful COMMIT. A SQLite failure is CMT_FAULT everywhere; a height ≤ 0 (the reference's panic) is CMT_FAULT; "not found" (ErrLightBlockNotFound) is CMT_OK with `*found = false`. Single owner, not thread-safe. Deviations (Kurultay #25 item 7): **D1** 64-bit count; **D2** re-saving a height replaces its bytes and keeps the count; **D3** rows and count in one transaction everywhere, Prune included; **D4** count changes = rows really inserted/deleted (`sqlite3_changes`); **D5** one count per prefix; **D6** open refuses a stored count that differs from the rows; **D7** a row whose block has no header or another height is CMT_REJECT; **D8** `block_protocol` at open; **D9** (K25-10, operator 2026-10-19; `cmt_light_store.h:96-119`) the ATTACK RECORDS — the reference's store has no such table (store.go:5-48) and its client no latch (detector.go:72, client.go:820-821). Each record the light client writes is kept as OPAQUE bytes (layout `cmt_light_attack_record_encode`, §25.10; this module never parses them); `seq` = 1, 2, … per prefix in save order; a record is NEVER deleted — the operator's clear sets `cleared = 1` on every record of the prefix, and the LOCK is "at least one record with `cleared = 0`"; one save or one clear is ONE transaction; saving bytes identical to a record NOT cleared adds nothing and returns that record's seq (after a clear the same bytes are a new row); records are not counted in Size() and Prune never touches them; ONE light-client object per prefix is the rule (`cmt_light_client.h` D20).

|------|-------------|
| `typedef struct { void *ctx; int (*save_light_block)(void *ctx, const cmt_light_block_t *lb); int (*delete_light_block)(void *ctx, int64_t height); int (*light_block)(void *ctx, int64_t height, cmt_light_block_buf_t *out, bool *found); int (*last_light_block_height)(void *ctx, int64_t *out); int (*first_light_block_height)(void *ctx, int64_t *out); int (*light_block_before)(void *ctx, int64_t height, cmt_light_block_buf_t *out, bool *found); int (*prune)(void *ctx, uint64_t size); int (*size)(void *ctx, uint64_t *out); int (*save_attack_record)(void *ctx, const uint8_t *bz, size_t len, uint64_t *seq); int (*attack_records)(void *ctx, uint64_t *total, uint64_t *uncleared); int (*clear_attack_records)(void *ctx); } cmt_light_store_t;` | **NEW; three rows added by the K25-10 fix (`cmt_light_store.h:200-213`).** The `Store` interface (store.go:5-48) as a callback table over `ctx` (the `cmt_mem_app_t` shape), plus the PROJECT D9 rows: save one attack record (`len` > 0 opaque bytes, `*seq` its sequence number, idempotent while not cleared), count the records (`*total`) and the uncleared ones (`*uncleared`; > 0 is the lock), and the operator's clear (every record cleared, none deleted). Every row required — the client constructors refuse a table with any NULL row, the three attack rows included (`cmt_light_client.c:1713-1715`). Empty store: last / first height -1. `prune` / `size` 64-bit (D1). |

CMT_OK; CMT_REJECT for a prefix above `CMT_LIGHT_STORE_PREFIX_MAX` or a count mismatch; CMT_FAULT on NULL, allocation or any SQLite failure. |

Height ≤ 0 → CMT_FAULT; none → CMT_OK, `*found = false`. |

CMT_OK; CMT_REJECT for `len` 0, `len` above `INT_MAX` (an SQLite bind takes an int) or a seq that would pass `INT64_MAX`; CMT_FAULT on NULL or any SQLite failure (a failed COMMIT is rolled back, `tx_finish`). |

On (CMT_OK, `*found`) `*out` is a malloc'd copy of its `*len` bytes (free() it) and `*cleared` its flag; otherwise `*out` is NULL. An absent seq (0 or above `INT64_MAX` included) is CMT_OK with `*found = false`. CMT_FAULT on NULL, an allocation failure, an empty stored value or any SQLite failure. |
